. Then, by the mean value theorem and the chain rule, there is
\varsigma\in[0,1]
such that
H(1)-H(0)=H^{\prime}(\varsigma)=\sum^{m}_{i=1}\frac{\partial}{\partial t_{i}}\,G[F(\varsigma)]\,\Delta_{i}
. This shows (
B.104
).

The following lemma is used in the proofs of Proposition
5.1
/Theorem
3.1
and Theorem
3.2
(in the former case, implicitly by means of the auxiliary results on matrices of the general form
\widetilde{{\mathbf{W}}}(a(n)2^{j})
).

Lemma B.11

Fix any
j
as in (
2.26
). Assume (
A1-A4
) hold. Let
\mathbf{W}_{X,Z}(a(n)2^{j})
be as in (
2.27
). Then,

\|a(n)^{-{({\mathbf{H}}+\frac{1}{2}{\mathbf{I}})}}\mathbf{W}_{X,Z}(a(n)2^{j}))\|=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

(B.105)

Proof:

For simplicity, write
\mathbf{W}_{Z,X}(a2^{j})=\mathbf{W}_{X,Z}(a2^{j})^{*}
. For any
{\mathbf{u}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{p}
,

0\leq{\mathbf{u}}^{*}\mathbf{W}_{Z,X}(a2^{j})a^{-{({\mathbf{H}}+\frac{1}{2}{\mathbf{I}})^{*}}}a^{-{({\mathbf{H}}+\frac{1}{2}{\mathbf{I}})}}\mathbf{W}_{X,Z}(a2^{j}){\mathbf{u}}

(B.106)

=\frac{1}{n_{a,j}^{2}}\sum_{k=1}^{n_{a,j}}\sum_{k^{\prime}=1}^{n_{a,j}}{\mathbf{u}}^{*}D_{Z}(a2^{j},k)D_{X}(a2^{j},k)^{*}a^{-{({\mathbf{H}}+\frac{1}{2}{\mathbf{I}})}^{*}}a^{-{({\mathbf{H}}+\frac{1}{2}{\mathbf{I}})}}D_{X}(a2^{j},k^{\prime})D_{Z}(a2^{j},k^{\prime})^{*}{\mathbf{u}}

=\frac{1}{n_{a,j}^{2}}\sum_{k=1}^{n_{a,j}}\sum_{k^{\prime}=1}^{n_{a,j}}{\mathbf{u}}^{*}D_{Z}(a2^{j},k)D_{Z}(a2^{j},k^{\prime})^{*}{\mathbf{u}}\Big\langle a^{-{({\mathbf{H}}+\frac{1}{2}{\mathbf{I}})}}D_{X}(a2^{j},k),a^{-{({\mathbf{H}}+\frac{1}{2}{\mathbf{I}})}}D_{X}(a2^{j},k^{\prime})\Big\rangle

\leq\frac{1}{n_{a,j}^{2}}\Bigg[\sum_{k=1}^{n_{a,j}}\sum_{k^{\prime}=1}^{n_{a,j}}\bigg({\mathbf{u}}^{*}D_{Z}(a2^{j},k)D_{Z}(a2^{j},k^{\prime})^{*}{\mathbf{u}}\bigg)^{2}

\times\sum_{\ell=1}^{n_{a,j}}\sum_{\ell^{\prime}=1}^{n_{a,j}}\Big\langle a^{-{({\mathbf{H}}+\frac{1}{2}{\mathbf{I}})}}D_{X}(a2^{j},\ell),a^{-{({\mathbf{H}}+\frac{1}{2}{\mathbf{I}})}}D_{X}(a2^{j},\ell^{\prime})\Big\rangle^{2}\Bigg]^{1/2}

=\frac{1}{n_{a,j}}\sum_{k=1}^{n_{a,j}}\bigg(D_{Z}(a2^{j},k)^{*}\mathbf{u}\bigg)^{2}\Bigg[\frac{1}{n_{a,j}^{2}}\sum_{\ell=1}^{n_{a,j}}\sum_{\ell^{\prime}=1}^{n_{a,j}}\Big\langle a^{-{({\mathbf{H}}+\frac{1}{2}{\mathbf{I}})}}D_{X}(a2^{j},\ell),a^{-{({\mathbf{H}}+\frac{1}{2}{\mathbf{I}})}}D_{X}(a2^{j},\ell^{\prime})\Big\rangle^{2}\Bigg]^{1/2},

where the second inequality is a consequence of the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality. However,

\frac{1}{n_{a,j}}\sum_{k=1}^{n_{a,j}}\bigg(D_{Z}(a2^{j},k)^{*}\mathbf{u}\bigg)^{2}=\frac{1}{n_{a,j}}\sum_{k=1}^{n_{a,j}}\mathbf{u}^{*}D_{Z}(a2^{j},k)D_{Z}(a2^{j},k)^{*}\mathbf{u}=\mathbf{u}^{*}\mathbf{W}_{Z}(a2^{j})\mathbf{u}\leq O_{{\mathbb{P}}}(1),

(B.107)

where the last equality results from (
2.29
). Moreover,

\Bigg[\frac{1}{n_{a,j}^{2}}\sum_{\ell=1}^{n_{a,j}}\sum_{\ell^{\prime}=1}^{n_{a,j}}\Big\langle a^{-{({\mathbf{H}}+\frac{1}{2}{\mathbf{I}})}}D_{X}(a2^{j},\ell),a^{-{({\mathbf{H}}+\frac{1}{2}{\mathbf{I}})}}D_{X}(a2^{j},\ell^{\prime})\Big\rangle^{2}\Bigg]^{1/2}

\leq\Bigg[\frac{1}{n_{a,j}^{2}}\sum_{\ell=1}^{n_{a,j}}\sum_{\ell^{\prime}=1}^{n_{a,j}}\Big\|a^{-{({\mathbf{H}}+\frac{1}{2}{\mathbf{I}})}}D_{X}(a2^{j},\ell)\Big\|^{2}\Big\|a^{-{({\mathbf{H}}+\frac{1}{2}{\mathbf{I}})}}D_{X}(a2^{j},\ell^{\prime})\Big\|^{2}\Bigg]^{1/2}

=\frac{1}{n_{a,j}}\sum_{\ell=1}^{n_{a,j}}\Big\|a^{-{({\mathbf{H}}+\frac{1}{2}{\mathbf{I}})}}D_{X}(a2^{j},\ell)\Big\|^{2}=\text{tr}\bigg(a^{-{({\mathbf{H}}+\frac{1}{2}{\mathbf{I}})}}\mathbf{W}_{X}(a2^{j})a^{-{({\mathbf{H}}+\frac{1}{2}{\mathbf{I}})^{*}}}\bigg)

\leq r\bigg\|a^{-{({\mathbf{H}}+\frac{1}{2}{\mathbf{I}})}}\mathbf{W}_{X}(a2^{j})a^{-{({\mathbf{H}}+\frac{1}{2}{\mathbf{I}})^{*}}}\bigg\|\leq r\|\mathbf{P}_{H}\|^{2}\|\widehat{\mathbf{B}}_{a}(2^{j})\|=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1),

(B.108)

where the last equality follows from (
2.30
) and (
2.35
). Thus, in view of (
B.107
) and (
B.108
), by taking
\sup_{\mathbf{u}\in\mathcal{S}^{p-1}}
in (
B.106
), we obtain the bound
\|a(n)^{-{({\mathbf{H}}+\frac{1}{2}{\mathbf{I}})}}\mathbf{W}_{X,Z}(a(n)2^{j})\|\leq O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
. This establishes (
B.105
).

Appendix C
Proofs: Section
4

In this section, we provide the proofs of statements made in Section
4
. The results are organized into two subsections, corresponding to Gaussian and non-Gaussian examples.

Hereinafter, for a stochastic process
Z
, we define the differencing operator by means of
\Delta Z(t):=Z(t)-Z(t-1)
. Likewise, the
k
–th order difference
\Delta^{k}Z=\Delta(\Delta^{k-1}Z)
is defined iteratively.

Consider the following definition.

Definition C.1

For each
p\in{\mathbb{N}}
, let
Z_{p}=Z=\{Z(t)\}_{t\in{\mathbb{Z}}}
be a second-order,
p
-variate process. We say the sequence of processes
\{Z_{p}\}_{p\in{\mathbb{N}}}
has
maximal memory parameter
d
uniformly in
p
(or
maximal order
d
, for short) if

(i)

for a fixed integer
k_{0}\geq 0
and each
p
, the
k_{0}
–th differenced process
\Delta^{k_{0}}Z
is (weakly) stationary with spectral density

|1-e^{{\mathbf{i}}x}|^{2k_{0}}\mathfrak{g}_{p}(x)\in{\mathcal{S}}_{\geq 0}(p,{\mathbb{C}}),\quad x\in[-\pi,\pi);

(C.1)

(ii)

there is a smallest
d\geq 0
satisfying
d-k_{0}\in(-1/2,1/2)
such that

\sup_{p\in{\mathbb{N}}}\hskip 5.69054pt\textnormal{ess}\sup_{|x|\leq\pi}|x|^{2d}\|\mathfrak{g}_{p}(x)\|<\infty.

(C.2)

For each
p
, the parameter
d
in (
C.2
) expresses the statement
\|\mathfrak{g}_{p}(x)\|=O(|x|^{-2d})
as
x\to 0
. Namely, it describes the largest possible scaling law in the behavior of
Z
. In particular, the condition
\textnormal{ess}\sup_{|x|\leq\pi}|x|^{2d}\|\mathfrak{g}_{p}(x)\|<\infty
includes spectral densities for multivariate long memory processes whose memory exponents are no greater than
d
(cf. Kechagias and Pipiras
[
kechagias:pipiras:2015:ident
]
). Further note that, for each
p
,
\mathfrak{g}_{p}
is typically called the
generalized spectral density
(of
Z
). This terminology is used in the sequel.

C.1
Section
4.1
: proofs and auxiliary results

In the following proposition, we show that all classes of examples described in Section
4.1
satisfy (
A2
) or (
A3
).

Proposition C.1

Suppose assumptions
(W1-W3)
are in place. Then, the following claims hold.

(i)

Fix
N_{\psi}\geq 2
and let
\vartheta_{0}:=\min\{2h_{1}+1,h_{1}+3/2\}>1
. Consider any dyadic sequence
\{a(n)\}_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}
such that

a(n)\leq\frac{n}{2^{j_{m}}},\quad\frac{a(n)}{n}+\frac{n}{a(n)^{\vartheta_{0}}}\rightarrow\infty,\quad n\rightarrow\infty.

(C.3)

Then, assumption
(A3)
is satisfied for
X=\{X(t)\}_{t\in{\mathbb{Z}}}
as in (
4.1
).

(ii)

For
N_{\psi}\geq 1
and under assumption
(A4)
, assumption
(A2)
is satisfied for
Z=\{Z(t)\}_{t\in{\mathbb{Z}}}
as in (
4.5
).

(iii)

For
d
as in (
4.8
), fix
N_{\psi}\geq 2
satisfying
N_{\psi}>d+\frac{1}{2}
. Suppose
\frac{p(n)}{n/a(n)}\to c>0
(cf. (
2.33
)) and, for
\alpha
as in (
A.3
), assume that
{na(n)^{-2\alpha}=O(1)}
. Also, fix

0<\varepsilon<\min\Big\{\frac{3}{2}(h_{1}+1/2),\,2h_{1}+1/2\Big\}-d.

(C.4)

Further define

b=h_{1}+1/2-\varepsilon.

(C.5)

Then, there exist sequences
\{a(n)\}_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}
and
\{p(n)\}_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}
such that assumptions (
A2
), (
A3
) and (
A4
) are satisfied (the latter, for large enough
n
) for
Z=\{Z(t)\}_{t\in{\mathbb{Z}}}
and
X=\{X(t)\}_{t\in{\mathbb{Z}}}
as in (
4.12
). In addition, the eigenvalues of the scaling matrix
\widetilde{{\mathbf{H}}}
(cf. (
1.5
)) for
X
are given by (
4.14
).

Proof:

We first show (
i
). Fix
N_{\psi}\geq 2
and let
\{a(n)\}_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}
be a dyadic sequence as in (
C.3
). Then, the associated auxiliary random matrix
\widehat{{\mathbf{B}}}_{a}(2^{j})
as in (
2.22
) satisfies assumption (
A3
) as a consequence of Theorem 3.1, Lemma C.2 (extended to dimension
r
) and Proposition 3.1 in Abry and Didier
[
abry:didier:2018:dim2
]
.

To show
(ii)
, consider
Z
as in (
4.5
). In this case,
Z
has spectral density

\mathfrak{g}_{p}(x)=\frac{1}{2\pi}\bigg(\sum_{\ell\in{\mathbb{Z}}}\mathbf{A}_{\ell}(p)e^{i\ell x}\bigg)\Sigma_{\varepsilon}(p)\bigg(\sum_{\ell\in{\mathbb{Z}}}\mathbf{A}_{\ell}(p)e^{i\ell x}\bigg)^{*}.

Thus,

\textnormal{ess}\sup_{|x|\leq\pi}\|\mathfrak{g}_{p}(x)\|\leq(2\pi)^{-1}\|\Sigma_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}(p)\|\bigg(\sum_{\ell\in{\mathbb{Z}}}\|\mathbf{A}_{\ell}(p)\|\bigg)^{2}.

Together with condition (
4.4
), this implies that
Z
is of maximal order
d=0
as in Definition
C.1
. Hence, by Lemma
C.1
, assumption (
A2
) holds under
(A4)
.

We now turn to (
iii
). Consider
Z
as in (
4.12
). We break up the proof into two cases, based on the magnitude

\eta_{0}:=2(d-h_{1}+\varepsilon)

(C.6)

(
n.b.
: relation (
C.7
) below shows that
2(d-h_{1}+\varepsilon)
naturally appears in the expression for the order of magnitude of
\|\mathbf{W}_{Z}(a(n)2^{j})\|
).

First, assume
\eta_{0}\leq 1
. For the fixed
c>0
, pick any sequences
\{a(n)\}_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}
and
\{p(n)\}_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}
such that assumption (
A4
) and relation (
C.3
) are satisfied. Since
\mathcal{Z}
is of maximal order
d
, and
N_{\psi}>d+1/2
, then Lemma
C.1
gives
\|\mathbf{W}_{\mathcal{Z}}(a(n)2^{j})\|=O_{{\mathbb{P}}}(a(n)^{2d})
. By (
C.5
) and
n/a(n)=O(p(n))
(see (
2.33
)), we obtain

\|\mathbf{W}_{Z}(a(n)2^{j})\|=\frac{\|\mathbf{W}_{\mathcal{Z}}(a(n)2^{j})\|}{p(n)\hskip 2.84526pta(n)^{2b}}=\frac{O_{\mathbb{P}}(a(n)^{2d})}{(n/a(n))\hskip 2.84526pta(n)^{2b}}

=O_{{\mathbb{P}}}(a(n)^{2(d-b)+1}/n)=O_{{\mathbb{P}}}(a(n)^{2(d-h_{1}+\varepsilon)}/n)=O_{{\mathbb{P}}}(1).

(C.7)

In (
C.7
), we used the fact that

\frac{a(n)^{2(d-h_{1}+\varepsilon)}}{n}=O(1).

(C.8)

In addition, by a similar reasoning,

\|{\mathbb{E}}\mathbf{W}_{Z}(a(n)2^{j})\|=O_{{\mathbb{P}}}(1).

(C.9)

Hence, assumption (
A2
) holds.

Moreover, for
b
as in (
C.5
), let
\widetilde{{\mathbf{H}}}={\mathbf{H}}-b\hskip 1.42262pt{\mathbf{I}}
. For
{\mathbf{W}}_{X}(a(n)2^{j})
as in (

), define the auxiliary random matrix

\widehat{{\mathbf{B}}}_{a}(2^{j})_{X}={\mathbf{P}}_{H}^{-1}\big\{a(n)^{-\widetilde{{\mathbf{H}}}-(1/2){\mathbf{I}}}\hskip 2.84526pt{\mathbf{W}}_{X}(a(n)2^{j})\hskip 2.84526pta(n)^{-\widetilde{{\mathbf{H}}}^{*}-(1/2){\mathbf{I}}}\big\}({\mathbf{P}}_{H}^{*})^{-1}\in{\mathcal{S}}_{\geq 0}(r,{\mathbb{R}}).

(C.10)

Also, let
\widehat{{\mathbf{B}}}_{a}(2^{j})_{{\mathcal{X}}}
be the auxiliary random matrix associated with the process
{\mathcal{X}}
as in (
4.6
). Then, in view of (
4.12
), relation (
4.14
) holds and

\widehat{{\mathbf{B}}}_{a}(2^{j})_{X}=\widehat{{\mathbf{B}}}_{a}(2^{j})_{{\mathcal{X}}}.

(C.11)

Thus, since

N_{\psi}\geq 2,

(C.12)

part
(i)
implies that (
A3
) holds (in particular, using the same rate of convergence
\sqrt{n_{a,j}}
).

Alternatively, assume

\eta_{0}>1.

(C.13)

Observe that (
C.4
) further gives

\eta_{0}<\min\big\{h_{1}+3/2,~2h_{1}+1\big\}.

(C.14)

For the fixed
c>0
, further define

a(n):=2^{\lfloor\frac{1}{\eta_{0}}\log_{2}n\rfloor},\quad p(n):=\Big\lfloor\frac{c\hskip 1.13809ptn}{a(n)}\Big\rfloor.

(C.15)

We claim that there are constants
0<C_{1}<C_{2}<\infty
such that

C_{1}\leq\frac{a(n)^{\eta_{0}}}{n}\leq C_{2},\quad n\in{\mathbb{N}}.

(C.16)

In fact, (
C.16
) is a consequence of exponentiating the inequalities

-\eta_{0}\leq\eta_{0}\Big(\Big\lfloor\frac{1}{\eta_{0}}\log_{2}n\Big\rfloor-\frac{1}{\eta_{0}}\log_{2}n\Big)\leq\eta_{0},\quad n\in{\mathbb{N}}.

As a consequence of (
C.16
), relation (
C.8
) holds. By the same argument, (
C.7
) also holds. Furthermore, a similar reasoning establishes (
C.9
). In other words, (

) is satisfied, as claimed.

Moreover, for
a(n)
and
p(n)
as in (

),
\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\frac{p(n)\hskip 0.56905pta(n)}{n}=c
. Also, by relations (

), (

), (

) and (

),
\frac{n}{a(n)^{h_{1}+3/2}}\leq\frac{C^{-1}_{1}}{a(n)^{h_{1}+3/2-\eta_{0}}}\rightarrow 0
as

as in (

), expression (

) implies

, giving

\frac{n}{a(n)^{\vartheta_{0}}}\leq\frac{n}{a(n)^{\eta_{0}}}a(n)^{\eta_{0}-\vartheta_{0}}\leq Ca(n)^{\eta_{0}-\vartheta_{0}}\to 0,\quad n\rightarrow\infty.

Thus, for
\widehat{{\mathbf{B}}}_{a}(2^{j})_{X}
as in (
C.10
), again relations (
C.11
) and (

) combined with part

show that assumption
(A3)
is satisfied. Furthermore, as in the previous case
\eta_{0}\leq 1
, relation (
4.14
) holds for

. This concludes the proof of

.
\Box

In the remainder of this section, we state or establish all the auxiliary results needed in the proof of Proposition
C.1
, namely, Lemmas
C.1
–
C.4
.

In the following lemma, we provide a bound on
\|{\mathbf{W}}_{Z}(a(n)2^{j})\|
and
\|{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbf{W}}_{Z}(a(n)2^{j})\|
under conditions on the underlying noise process
Z
.

Lemma C.1

Suppose assumptions
(W1-W3)
and
(A4)
are in place. For each
p=p(n)
, let
Z=\{Z(t)\}_{t\in{\mathbb{Z}}}
be a Gaussian,
p
-variate stochastic processes satisfying Definition
C.1
. If, in addition,
N_{\psi}>d+\frac{1}{2}
and
na(n)^{-2\alpha}=O(1)
, then

a(n)^{-2d}\|\mathbf{W}_{Z}(a(n)2^{j})\|=O_{{\mathbb{P}}}(1),\quad\textnormal{and}\quad a(n)^{-2d}\|{\mathbb{E}}\mathbf{W}_{Z}(a(n)2^{j})\|=O(1).

(C.17)

Proof:

The second relation in (
C.17
) is a consequence of (
C.43
) (see Lemma
C.3
).

So, we show the first relation in (
C.17
). Define

\mathcal{H}_{2^{j}}(x):=\sum_{\ell\in{\mathbb{Z}}}h_{j,\ell}e^{-{\mathbf{i}}x\ell}.

(C.18)

By relation (16) in Moulines et al.
[
moulines:roueff:taqqu:2007:JTSA
]
, p. 161, we can express
\mathcal{H}_{2^{j}}(x)=(1-e^{-{\mathbf{i}}x})^{N_{\psi}}\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{2^{j}}(x)
, where
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{2^{j}}(x)
is a trigonometric polynomial. Since
N_{\psi}\geq d-1/2
, expression (17) in Moulines et al.
[
moulines:roueff:taqqu:2007:JTSA
]
and the fact that
Z
is Gaussian imply that the wavelet coefficients
D_{Z}(2^{j},k)
are well defined a.s., are Gaussian and satisfy

{\mathbb{E}}D_{Z}(2^{j},k)D_{Z}(2^{j^{\prime}},k^{\prime})^{*}=\int^{\pi}_{-\pi}e^{{\mathbf{i}}x(2^{j}k-2^{j^{\prime}}k^{\prime})}\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{2^{j}}(x)\overline{\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{2^{j^{\prime}}}(x)}|1-e^{{\mathbf{i}}x}|^{2N_{\psi}}\mathfrak{g}_{p}(x)\hskip 1.42262ptdx

=\int^{\pi}_{-\pi}e^{{\mathbf{i}}x(2^{j}k-2^{j^{\prime}}k^{\prime})}{\mathcal{H}}_{2^{j}}(x)\overline{{\mathcal{H}}_{2^{j^{\prime}}}(x)}\hskip 1.42262pt\mathfrak{g}_{p}(x)\hskip 1.42262ptdx.

(C.19)

Thus, the wavelet random matrices
{\mathbf{W}}_{Z}(a(n)2^{j})
are well defined a.s. Then, the claim is a consequence of Lemma
C.2
(see expression (
C.27
)).
\Box

We now set up some notation for the next lemma. For a fixed
\mathbf{u}\in\mathcal{S}^{p-1}
, let

V_{n}(\mathbf{u}):=\Big(D_{Z}(a2^{j},1)^{*}{\mathbf{u}},D_{Z}(a2^{j},2)^{*}{\mathbf{u}},\ldots,D_{Z}(a2^{j},n_{a,j})^{*}{\mathbf{u}}\Big)^{*}

(C.20)

be the vector of available wavelet coefficients of
Z
at scale
a(n)2^{j}
projected in the direction
\mathbf{u}
. Still for
\mathbf{u}\in\mathcal{S}^{p-1}
, let

\mathbf{\Gamma}_{n}(\mathbf{u})={\mathbb{E}}V_{n}(\mathbf{u})V_{n}(\mathbf{u})^{*}.

(C.21)

Also, define

\beta_{0}:=\limsup_{n\rightarrow\infty}a(n)^{-2d}\sup_{\|\mathbf{u}\|=1}\|\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{n}(\mathbf{u})\|.

(C.22)

Note that, by Lemma
C.3
,
\beta_{0}<\infty
.
In the following lemma, we establish a concentration inequality for the norm
\|(a(n)2^{j})^{-2d}{\mathbf{W}}_{Z}(a(n)2^{j})\|=\lambda_{p}\big((a(n)2^{j})^{-2d}{\mathbf{W}}_{Z}(a(n)2^{j})\big)
. The proof follows an
\varepsilon
-net argument, involving steps of approximation, concentration and union bound (cf. Vershynin
[
vershynin:2018
]
, Sections 4.4 and 4.6, or Lugosi
[
lugosi:2017
]
, pp. 13–14).

Lemma C.2

Suppose the assumptions of Lemma
C.1
hold. Let

m(a(n)2^{j})=\|{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbf{W}}_{Z}(a(n)2^{j})\|.

(C.23)

With
\beta_{0}
as in (
C.22
), take any
\beta>\beta_{0}
, and let

t\geq 5\beta>5\beta_{0}.

(C.24)

Then, for large enough
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
,

{\mathbb{P}}\Big((a(n)2^{j})^{-2d}\big(\|{\mathbf{W}}_{Z}(a(n)2^{j})\|-m(a(n)2^{j})\big)>t\Big)

\leq\exp\left\{p(n)\log 9-\frac{n}{a(n)2^{j}}\left(\frac{t-\beta}{4\beta}-\sqrt{\frac{t-\beta}{4\beta}}\right)\right\}.

(C.25)

In particular, let
C
be any constant satisfying

C>\beta\bigg(2+\left(1+\sqrt{1+2^{j+2}c\log 9}\right)^{2}\bigg),

(C.26)

where
c\geq 0
is as in (
2.33
). Then, as
n\rightarrow\infty
,

\lambda_{p}\big((2^{j}a(n))^{-2d}{\mathbf{W}}_{Z}(a(n)2^{j})\big)<C

(C.27)

with probability going to 1.

Proof:

So, fix a small
\varepsilon>0
. Also fix
{\mathbf{u}}\in\mathcal{S}^{p-1}
and let

{\mathcal{D}}^{*}_{Z,n}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mathbf{u}^{*}{\mathbb{E}}\mathbf{W}_{Z}(a2^{j})\mathbf{u}}}\Big(D_{Z}(a2^{j},1)^{*}{\mathbf{u}},D_{Z}(a2^{j},2)^{*}{\mathbf{u}},\ldots,D_{Z}(a2^{j},n_{a,j})^{*}{\mathbf{u}}\Big)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n_{a,j}}.

(C.28)

Write

{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{{\mathcal{D}},n}={\mathbb{E}}{\mathcal{D}}_{Z,n}{\mathcal{D}}^{*}_{Z,n}

and consider its spectral decomposition
{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{{\mathcal{D}},n}=\mathbf{O}_{n}\mathbf{\Lambda}_{{\mathcal{D}},n}\mathbf{O}^{*}_{n}
for an orthogonal matrix
\mathbf{O}_{n}
. Recast

\frac{1}{n_{a,j}}{\mathcal{D}}^{*}_{Z,n}{\mathcal{D}}_{Z,n}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\frac{1}{n_{a,j}}{\mathbf{Z}}^{*}_{n}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{{\mathcal{D}},n}{\mathbf{Z}}_{n}=\frac{1}{n_{a,j}}{\mathbf{Z}}^{*}_{n}\mathbf{O}_{n}\mathbf{\Lambda}_{{\mathcal{D}},n}\mathbf{O}^{*}_{n}{\mathbf{Z}}_{n}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\frac{1}{n_{a,j}}{\mathbf{Z}}^{*}_{n}\mathbf{\Lambda}_{{\mathcal{D}},n}{\mathbf{Z}}_{n}=:\sum^{n_{a,j}}_{k=1}\eta_{k,n}Z^{2}_{k},

where
\mathbf{Z}_{n}=(Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{n_{a,j}})^{*}
is a vector of i.i.d. standard normal random variables. Let

{\boldsymbol{\eta}}={\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{n}=\Big(\eta_{1,n},\ldots,\eta_{n_{a,j},n}\Big)

(C.29)

be the vector of eigenvalues of the deterministic matrix
\frac{1}{n_{a,j}}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{{\mathcal{D}},n}
. Note that, by the stationarity of
\{D_{Z}(2^{j},k)\}_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}}
(see (
C.19
)),

\mathbf{u}^{*}{\mathbb{E}}\mathbf{W}_{Z}(a2^{j})\mathbf{u}=\frac{1}{n_{a,j}}\sum_{k=1}^{n_{a,j}}{\mathbb{E}}\big(\mathbf{u}^{*}D_{Z}(a2^{j},k)D_{Z}(a2^{j},k)^{*}\mathbf{u}\big)={\mathbb{E}}\big(D_{Z}(a2^{j},1)^{*}{\mathbf{u}}\big)^{2}.

Hence,

\frac{1}{n_{a,j}}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathcal{D}}^{*}_{Z,n}{\mathcal{D}}_{Z,n}=\frac{1}{n_{a,j}{\mathbf{u}^{*}{\mathbb{E}}\mathbf{W}_{Z}(a2^{j})\mathbf{u}}}\cdot n_{a,j}{\mathbb{E}}\big(D_{Z}(a2^{j},1)^{*}{\mathbf{u}}\big)^{2}=1,

whence

\sum_{i=1}^{n_{a,j}}\eta_{i,n}=1

(C.30)

On the other hand, let

m_{n}(\mathbf{u}):=(a2^{j})^{-2d}{\mathbf{u}}^{*}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbf{W}}_{Z}(a2^{j}){\mathbf{u}}.

In particular,

\sup_{\|\mathbf{u}\|=1}m_{n}(\mathbf{u})=\frac{m(a2^{j})}{(a2^{j})^{2d}}.

(C.31)

Recall that
\mathbf{\Gamma}_{n}(\mathbf{u})
is defined by (
C.21
). Then, by Lemma
C.3
, for all large
n
,

\|\boldsymbol{\eta}\|_{\infty}=\frac{1}{n_{a,j}}\|{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{{\mathcal{D}},n}\|=\frac{\|\mathbf{\Gamma}_{n}(\mathbf{u})\|}{n_{a,j}{\mathbf{u}}^{*}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbf{W}}_{Z}(a2^{j}){\mathbf{u}}}\leq\frac{\beta a^{2d}}{n_{a,j}{\mathbf{u}}^{*}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbf{W}}_{Z}(a2^{j}){\mathbf{u}}}=\frac{\beta}{n_{a,j}m_{n}(\mathbf{u})}.

(C.32)

So, for
\beta
as in (
C.24
), consider any
s>0
such that

s>2\beta.

(C.33)

Define

x=\left(\frac{-\|\boldsymbol{\eta}\|_{2}+\sqrt{\|\boldsymbol{\eta}\|_{2}^{2}+2\|\boldsymbol{\eta}\|_{\infty}s/m_{n}(\mathbf{u})}}{2\|\boldsymbol{\eta}\|_{\infty}}\right)^{2}.

Then,
2\|{\boldsymbol{\eta}}\|_{2}\hskip 2.84526pt\sqrt{x}+2\|{\boldsymbol{\eta}}\|_{\infty}\hskip 2.84526ptx=s/m_{n}(\mathbf{u})
.
Thus, using (
C.31
) and (
C.30
),

{\mathbb{P}}\bigg((a2^{j})^{-2d}\Big({\mathbf{u}}^{*}{\mathbf{W}}_{Z}(a2^{j}){\mathbf{u}}-m(a2^{j})\Big)>s\bigg)

\leq{\mathbb{P}}\bigg((a2^{j})^{-2d}\Big({\mathbf{u}}^{*}{\mathbf{W}}_{Z}(a2^{j}){\mathbf{u}}-{\mathbf{u}}^{*}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbf{W}}_{Z}(a2^{j}){\mathbf{u}}\Big)>s\bigg)

={\mathbb{P}}\Big(\frac{{\mathbf{u}}^{*}{\mathbf{W}}_{Z}(a2^{j}){\mathbf{u}}}{{\mathbf{u}}^{*}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbf{W}}_{Z}(a2^{j})\mathbf{\mathbf{u}}}-1>\frac{s}{(a2^{j})^{-2d}{\mathbf{u}}^{*}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbf{W}}_{Z}(a2^{j})\mathbf{\mathbf{u}}}\Big)={\mathbb{P}}\Big(\sum^{n_{a,j}}_{k=1}\eta_{k,n}(Z^{2}_{k}-1)\geq\frac{s}{m_{n}(\mathbf{u})}\Big)

={\mathbb{P}}\Big(\sum^{n_{a,j}}_{k=1}\eta_{k,n}(Z^{2}_{k}-1)\geq 2\|{\boldsymbol{\eta}}\|_{2}\hskip 2.84526pt\sqrt{x}+2\|{\boldsymbol{\eta}}\|_{\infty}\hskip 2.84526ptx\Big)\leq\exp\{-x\},

(C.34)

where the last inequality is a consequence of Lemma
C.4
.

Now, note that
\|\boldsymbol{\eta}\|_{2}\leq\sqrt{n_{a,j}}\|\boldsymbol{\eta}\|_{\infty}
and that
\sqrt{\|\boldsymbol{\eta}\|_{2}^{2}+2\|\boldsymbol{\eta}\|_{\infty}s/m_{n}(\mathbf{u})}\leq\|\boldsymbol{\eta}\|_{2}+\sqrt{2\|\boldsymbol{\eta}\|_{\infty}s/m_{n}(\mathbf{u})}
. We obtain

x=\frac{1}{2\|\boldsymbol{\eta}\|_{\infty}^{2}}\Bigg(\|\boldsymbol{\eta}\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{\|\boldsymbol{\eta}\|_{\infty}s}{m_{n}(\mathbf{u})}-\|\boldsymbol{\eta}\|_{2}\sqrt{\|\boldsymbol{\eta}\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{2\|\boldsymbol{\eta}\|_{\infty}s}{m_{n}(\mathbf{u})}}\Bigg)

\geq\frac{1}{2\|\boldsymbol{\eta}\|_{\infty}^{2}}\Bigg(\frac{\|\boldsymbol{\eta}\|_{\infty}s}{m_{n}(\mathbf{u})}-\|\boldsymbol{\eta}\|_{2}\sqrt{\frac{2\|\boldsymbol{\eta}\|_{\infty}s}{m_{n}(\mathbf{u})}}\Bigg)

\geq\frac{1}{\|\boldsymbol{\eta}\|_{\infty}}\left(\frac{s}{2m_{n}(\mathbf{u})}-\sqrt{n_{a,j}\frac{s\|\boldsymbol{\eta}\|_{\infty}}{2m_{n}(\mathbf{u})}}\right)

\geq\frac{1}{\|\boldsymbol{\eta}\|_{\infty}}\left(\frac{s}{2m_{n}(\mathbf{u})}-\sqrt{\frac{s\beta}{2m^{2}_{n}(\mathbf{u})}}\right)=\frac{1}{\|\boldsymbol{\eta}\|_{\infty}m_{n}(\mathbf{u})}\left(\frac{s}{2}-\sqrt{\frac{s\beta}{2}}\right)

\geq\frac{n_{a,j}}{\beta}\left(\frac{s}{2}-\sqrt{\frac{s\beta}{2}}\right).

(C.35)

In (
C.35
), the third inequality follows from relation (
C.32
), and the last inequality holds since
s/2-\sqrt{{s\beta}/{2}}>0
due to (
C.33
). Recall that
m(a2^{j})
is given by (
C.23
). From (
C.34
) and (
C.35
), we arrive at

{\mathbb{P}}\Big((a2^{j})^{-2d}\Big({\mathbf{u}}^{*}{\mathbf{W}}_{Z}(a2^{j}){\mathbf{u}}-m(a2^{j})\Big)>s\Big)\leq\exp\left\{-n_{a,j}\left(\frac{s}{2\beta}-\sqrt{\frac{s}{2\beta}}\right)\right\}.

(C.36)

We now appeal to the same argument as in Lugosi
[
lugosi:2017
]
, pp. 13–14. In fact, let
\mathcal{N}
be a
1/4
-net of the unit sphere. Then, it can be shown that
\text{card}(\mathcal{N})\leq 9^{p}
. By an application of the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we obtain
\|{\mathbf{W}}_{Z}(a2^{j})\|\leq 2\max_{\mathbf{u}\in\mathcal{N}}{\mathbf{u}}^{*}{\mathbf{W}}_{Z}(a2^{j}){\mathbf{u}}
. Let
t
be as in
(
C.24
)
. Thus, by the union bound,

{\mathbb{P}}\Big((a2^{j})^{-2d}\big(\|\mathbf{W}_{Z}(a2^{j})\|-m(a2^{j})\big)>t\Big)\leq 9^{p}\max_{{\mathbf{u}}\in{\mathcal{N}}}{\mathbb{P}}\Big({\mathbf{u}}^{*}{\mathbf{W}}_{Z}(a2^{j}){\mathbf{u}}>\frac{(a2^{j})^{2d}t+m(a2^{j})}{2}\Big)

=9^{p}\max_{{\mathbf{u}}\in{\mathcal{N}}}{\mathbb{P}}\Big((a2^{j})^{-2d}\big({\mathbf{u}}^{*}{\mathbf{W}}_{Z}(a2^{j}){\mathbf{u}}-m(a2^{j})\big)>\frac{t-(a2^{j})^{-2d}m(a2^{j})}{2}\Big).

(C.37)

Recall expression (
C.23
). Note that, by relation (
C.43
) of Lemma
C.3
, since
\beta>\beta_{0}
, for all large
n
,

(a2^{j})^{-2d}m(a2^{j})<\beta.

(C.38)

In view of (
C.24
), the inequality
\frac{t-(a2^{j})^{-2d}m(a2^{j})}{2}>\frac{5\beta-\beta}{2}=2\beta
holds for all large
n
; i.e., (
C.33
) holds with
s=\frac{t-(a2^{j})^{-2d}m(a2^{j})}{2}
. Thus, applying (
C.36
) to (
C.37
), we get

{\mathbb{P}}\Big((a2^{j})^{-2d}(\|\mathbf{W}_{Z}(a2^{j})\|-m(a2^{j}))>t\Big)

\leq 9^{p}\exp\left\{-n_{a,j}\left(\frac{t-(a2^{j})^{-2d}m(a2^{j})}{4\beta}-\sqrt{\frac{t-(a2^{j})^{-2d}m(a2^{j})}{4\beta}}\right)\right\}.

\leq 9^{p}\exp\left\{-n_{a,j}\left(\frac{t-\beta}{4\beta}-\sqrt{\frac{t-\beta}{4\beta}}\right)\right\}.

(C.39)

In (
C.39
), we used that
v\mapsto v-\sqrt{v}
is increasing for all
v>1/4
, and that
\frac{t-(a2^{j})^{-2d}m(a2^{j})}{4\beta}>\frac{2\beta}{2\beta}>1
. By combining this with (
C.36
) and (
C.37
), we arrive at (
C.25
).

To show the statement regarding (
C.26
), recast

\exp\left\{p(n)\log 9-\frac{n}{a(n)2^{j}}\left(\frac{t-\beta}{4\beta}-\sqrt{\frac{t-\beta}{4\beta}}\right)\right\}=:\exp\left\{\frac{n}{a(n)}\hskip 1.42262ptb(n)\right\}.

(C.40)

By (
2.33
) and (
C.38
),

b(n)=\frac{p(n)}{n/a(n)}\log 9-\frac{1}{2^{j}}\left(\frac{t-\beta}{4\beta}-\sqrt{\frac{t-\beta}{4\beta}}\right)

\rightarrow c\log 9-\frac{1}{2^{j}}\left(\frac{t-\beta}{4\beta}-\sqrt{\frac{t-\beta}{4\beta}}\right)=:L(t),\quad n\rightarrow\infty.

Thus, if
t>t_{*}:=\beta\bigg(1+\left(1+\sqrt{1+2^{j+2}c\log 9}\right)^{2}\bigg)
, then
L(t)<0
, implying
b(n)<0
for all large
n
. So, for
C
satisfying (
C.26
), we have

C>\beta+\beta\bigg(1+\left(1+\sqrt{1+2^{j+2}c\log 9}\right)^{2}\bigg)=\beta+t_{*}.

Thus, for some
t>t_{*}
, relation (
C.43
) (see Lemma
C.3
) implies that
C>\beta+t\geq(a2^{j})^{-2d}m(a2^{j})+t
for all large
n
. Also, for any fixed
t>t_{*}
,
L(t)<-2\delta<0
for some sufficiently small
\delta=\delta(t)>0
, giving
b(n)<-\delta
for all large
n
. Therefore, by (
C.25
) and (
C.40
),

{\mathbb{P}}\Big((a2^{j})^{-2d}\lambda_{p}\big({\mathbf{W}}_{Z}(a(n)2^{j})\big)>C\Big)\leq\exp\left\{\frac{n}{a(n)}\hskip 1.42262ptb(n)\right\}\leq\exp\left\{-\frac{n}{a(n)}\hskip 1.42262pt\delta\right\}\to 0,

as
n\rightarrow\infty
. Consequently,
\lambda_{p}\big({\mathbf{W}}_{Z}(a(n)2^{j})\big)
is bounded above by any such constant
C
with probability going to 1, as claimed.
\Box

The following lemma is used in the proof of Lemma
C.1
by means of Lemma
C.2
. In the lemma, we establish some properties of the wavelet coefficients of colored noise. To state the lemma, consider the expressions (
2.21
) and (
C.20
). Now let
\mathbf{u}_{0}
be a unit vector such that
\mathbf{u}_{0}^{*}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbf{W}}_{Z}(a(n)2^{j})\mathbf{u}_{0}=\|{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbf{W}}_{Z}(a(n)2^{j})\|
. Then, we can see that

\|{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbf{W}}_{Z}(a(n)2^{j})\|\textnormal{ appears along the diagonal of }\mathbf{\Gamma}_{n}(\mathbf{u}_{0}),

(C.41)

since, for any
k=1,\ldots,n_{a,j}
,

{\mathbb{E}}D_{Z}(a2^{j},k)^{*}\mathbf{u}_{0}{\mathbf{u}}^{*}_{0}D_{Z}(a2^{j},k)=\mathbf{u}_{0}^{*}{\mathbb{E}}D_{Z}(a2^{j},k)D_{Z}(a2^{j},k)^{*}{\mathbf{u}}_{0}.

Lemma C.3

Suppose the assumptions of Lemma
C.1
hold. For a fixed
\mathbf{u}\in\mathcal{S}^{p-1}
, let
\mathbf{\Gamma}_{n}(\mathbf{u})
be as in (
C.21
). Then, for some
C_{0}>0
that is independent of
p
,
n
and
j
,

\big(a(n)2^{j}\big)^{-2d}\sup_{\|\mathbf{u}\|=1}\|\mathbf{\Gamma}_{n}(\mathbf{u})\|\leq C_{0}.

(C.42)

In particular,

\big(a(n)2^{j}\big)^{-2d}\|{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbf{W}}_{Z}(a(n)2^{j})\|\leq C_{0}.

(C.43)

Proof:

Recall that any diagonal entry of
\Gamma_{n}(\mathbf{u})
is bounded above by the spectral norm
\|\Gamma_{n}(\mathbf{u})\|
. Thus, in view of (
C.41
), it suffices to establish the statement (
C.42
). For simplicity we consider
2^{j}=1
and write
n_{a,j}=n_{a}
; the full statement can be then obtained by simply replacing
a(n)
with
a(n)2^{j}
.

First observe, by (
C.19
), the
(k,k^{\prime})
–th entry of
\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{n}(\mathbf{u})
is given as

\mathbf{u}^{*}{\mathbb{E}}D_{Z}(a,k)D_{Z}(a,k^{\prime})^{*}\mathbf{u}=\int^{\pi}_{-\pi}e^{{\mathbf{i}}x(a(k-k^{\prime}))}|{{\mathcal{H}}_{a}(x)}|^{2}\big(\mathbf{u}^{*}\mathfrak{g}_{p}(x)\mathbf{u}\big)dx

=\frac{1}{a}\int^{\pi a}_{-\pi a}e^{{\mathbf{i}}y(k-k^{\prime})}|{{\mathcal{H}}_{a}(y/a)}|^{2}\big(\mathbf{u}^{*}\mathfrak{g}_{p}(y/a)\mathbf{u}\big)dy,

where we used the change-of-variable
x=y/a
on the second line. Also, recall from relation (78) of Moulines et al.
[
moulines:roueff:taqqu:2007:JTSA
]
,
for some
C_{1}>0
,

\big|{\mathcal{H}}_{a}(x)\big|\leq C_{1}a^{1/2}|ax|^{N_{\psi}}(1+a|x|)^{-\alpha-N_{\psi}},\quad x\in(-\pi,\pi),

where
\alpha
is given by (
A.3
). Thus, for all
|y/a|\leq\pi
, we have

|{\mathcal{H}}_{a}(y/a)|^{2}\big(\mathbf{u}^{*}\mathfrak{g}_{p}(y/a)\mathbf{u}\big)\leq|{\mathcal{H}}_{a}(y/a)|^{2}\hskip 1.42262pt\|\mathfrak{g}_{p}(y/a)\|

\leq C_{2}a|y|^{2N_{\psi}}(1+|y|)^{-2\alpha-2N_{\psi}}|y/a|^{-2d}\big(|y/a|^{2d}\|\mathfrak{g}_{p}(y/a)\|\big)

\leq C_{3}a^{(2d+1)}|y|^{2N_{\psi}-2d}(1+|y|)^{-2\alpha-2N_{\psi}}.

(C.44)

In (
C.44
),
C_{3}>0
is independent of
p
and the last inequality follows from (
C.2
). This gives, for any
\mathbf{v}=(v_{1},\ldots,v_{n_{a}})\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n_{a}}
with
\|\mathbf{v}\|=1
, and using again the change-of-variables
x=y/a
,

0\leq\mathbf{v}^{*}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{n}(\mathbf{u})\mathbf{v}=\int^{\pi}_{-\pi}\sum_{\ell=1}^{n_{a}}\sum_{\ell^{\prime}=1}^{n_{a}}v_{\ell}v_{\ell^{\prime}}e^{{\mathbf{i}}xa(\ell-\ell^{\prime})}|{\mathcal{H}}_{a}(x)|^{2}\big(\mathbf{u}^{*}\mathfrak{g}_{p}(x)\mathbf{u}\big)dx

=\frac{1}{a}\int^{\pi a}_{-\pi a}\Big|\sum_{\ell=1}^{n_{a}}v_{\ell}e^{{\mathbf{i}}y\ell}\Big|^{2}|{\mathcal{H}}_{a}(y/a)|^{2}\hskip 1.42262pt\big(\mathbf{u}^{*}\mathfrak{g}_{p}(y/a)\mathbf{u}\big)\hskip 1.42262ptdy

\leq C_{3}a^{2d}\int^{\pi a}_{-\pi a}\Big|\sum_{\ell=1}^{n_{a}}v_{\ell}e^{{\mathbf{i}}y\ell}\Big|^{2}\frac{|y|^{2N_{\psi}-2d}}{(1+|y|)^{2\alpha+2N_{\psi}}}dy

=C_{3}a^{2d}\sum_{\ell=1}^{n_{a}}\sum_{\ell^{\prime}=1}^{n_{a}}v_{\ell}v_{\ell^{\prime}}\int^{\pi a}_{-\pi a}e^{{\mathbf{i}}y(\ell-\ell^{\prime})}\frac{|y|^{2N_{\psi}-2d}}{(1+|y|)^{2\alpha+2N_{\psi}}}dy.

(C.45)

Starting from the argument of the integral in (
C.45
), let
g(y)=|y|^{2N_{\psi}-2d}(1+|y|)^{-2(\alpha+N_{\psi})}
. Since
2(N_{\psi}-d)>1
, then
g^{\prime\prime}
is integrable. Hence, its Fourier transform satisfies
|\widehat{g}(\ell)|=o(\ell^{-2})
as
|\ell|\to\infty
. Therefore,
\sum_{\ell\in{\mathbb{Z}}}|\widehat{g}(\ell)|<\infty
. Furthermore, consider the truncated function
g_{a}(y)=g(y){\mathbf{1}}_{\{|y|\leq\pi a\}}
as well as its Fourier transform
\widehat{g}_{a}
. Then, we can bound

|\widehat{g}_{a}(\ell)-\widehat{g}(\ell)|\leq 2\int_{\pi a}^{\infty}g(y)dy\leq C_{4}\frac{1}{a^{(2(\alpha+d)-1)}}.

Thus, after dividing through by
a^{2d}
, (
C.45
) is bounded, in absolute value, by

C_{3}\bigg|\sum_{\ell=1}^{n_{a}}\sum_{\ell^{\prime}=1}^{n_{a}}v_{\ell}v_{\ell^{\prime}}\widehat{g}_{a}(\ell-\ell^{\prime})\bigg|=C_{3}\bigg|\widehat{g}_{a}(0)+\sum_{m=1}^{n_{a}-1}2\Re(\widehat{g}_{a}(m))\sum_{\ell=1}^{n_{a}-m}v_{\ell}v_{\ell+m}\bigg|

\leq C_{5}\bigg(|\widehat{g}_{a}(0)|+2\sum_{m=1}^{n_{a}-1}|\widehat{g}_{a}(m)|\bigg)\leq C_{6}\bigg(\sum_{m=0}^{n_{a}-1}|\widehat{g}_{a}(m)|\bigg)\leq C_{6}\bigg(\sum_{m=0}^{n_{a}-1}|\widehat{g}_{a}(m)-\widehat{g}(m)|+\sum_{m=0}^{n_{a}-1}|\widehat{g}(m)|\bigg)

\leq C_{7}\bigg(\frac{n}{a^{2(\alpha+d)}}+\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}|\widehat{g}(m)|\bigg).

(C.46)

In (
C.46
), the first equality and inequality follow from
\sum_{\ell=1}^{n_{a}}v_{\ell}^{2}=1
and the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, respectively. Since
d\geq 0
(cf. (
C.2
)), using
na^{-2(\alpha+d)}\leq na^{-2\alpha}=O(1)
, we obtain (
C.42
).
\Box

The following lemma is used in the proof of Lemma
C.2
. It provides a concentration inequality for centered quadratic forms. It corresponds to Lemma 1 in Laurent and Massart
[
laurent:massart:2000
]
(see also Birgé and Massart
[
birge:massart:1998
]
, Lemma 8, and Boucheron et al.
[
boucheron:lugosi:massart:2013
]
, p. 39).

Lemma C.4

(Laurent and Massart
[
laurent:massart:2000
]
) Let
Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{n}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textnormal{i.i.d.}}}{{\sim}}{\mathcal{N}}(0,1)
and
\eta_{1},\ldots,\eta_{n}\geq 0
, not all zero. Let
\|{\boldsymbol{\eta}}\|_{2}
and
\|{\boldsymbol{\eta}}\|_{\infty}
be the Euclidean square and sup norms of the vector
{\boldsymbol{\eta}}=(\eta_{1},\ldots,\eta_{n})^{*}
. Also, define the random variable

X=\sum^{r}_{i=1}\eta_{i}(Z^{2}_{i}-1)
.
Then, for every
x>0
,

{\mathbb{P}}\Big(X\geq 2\|{\boldsymbol{\eta}}\|_{2}\hskip 2.84526pt\sqrt{x}+2\|{\boldsymbol{\eta}}\|_{\infty}\hskip 2.84526ptx\Big)\leq\exp\{-x\},

(C.47)

{\mathbb{P}}\Big(X\leq-2\|{\boldsymbol{\eta}}\|_{2}\hskip 2.84526pt\sqrt{x}\Big)\leq\exp\{-x\}.

(C.48)

C.2
Section
4.2
: auxiliary results for the case of non-Gaussian
X

The following proposition is mentioned in Section
4.2
. In the statement of the proposition, we make use of the following univariate construct. Suppose the univariate stochastic process
{\mathcal{X}}_{\tilde{d},k}=\{{\mathcal{X}}_{\tilde{d},k}(t)\}_{t\in{\mathbb{Z}}}
has memory parameter
\tilde{d}>0
(Roueff and Taqqu
[
roueff:taqqu:2009
]
, Definition 1). In other words, for any integer
k>\tilde{d}-1/2
, the
k
-th order difference process
\Delta^{k}{\mathcal{X}}_{\tilde{d},k}
is weakly stationary with spectral density

f_{\Delta{\mathcal{X}}_{\tilde{d},k}}(x)=|1-e^{-{\mathbf{i}}x}|^{2(k-\tilde{d})}f_{*}(x),\quad x\in[-\pi,\pi)

(C.49)

(cf. Definition
C.1
). In (
C.49
),
f_{*}(x)\geq 0
is a symmetric function that is continuous and nonzero at the origin. Moreover, we assume that, for some

\beta\in(0,2],

(C.50)

the function
f_{*}
satisfies

|f_{*}(x)-f_{*}(0)|=O(|x|^{\beta}),\quad x\to 0.

In addition, suppose
\Delta^{k}{\mathcal{X}}_{\tilde{d},k}(t)
is a linear process of the form

\Delta^{k}{\mathcal{X}}_{\tilde{d},k}(t)=\sum_{\ell\in{\mathbb{Z}}}a_{\tilde{d}}(t-\ell)\xi_{\ell},\quad\sum_{\ell\in{\mathbb{Z}}}a^{2}_{\tilde{d}}(\ell)<\infty.

(C.51)

In (
C.51
),
\{\xi_{\ell}\}_{\ell\in{\mathbb{Z}}}
is a sequence of i.i.d. random variables with mean zero, unit variance, and finite fourth moment.

Proposition C.2

Let
0<\tilde{d}_{1}\ldots\leq\tilde{d}_{r}
. Fix
k_{\ell}\in{\mathbb{N}}\cup\{0\}
,
\ell=1,\ldots,r
, and
\beta
satisfying (
C.50
). Further fix pairwise distinct
j_{1},\ldots,j_{m}\in{\mathbb{N}}\cup\{0\}
. Suppose the following conditions are in place.

(i)

X=\{X(t)\}_{t\in{\mathbb{Z}}}=\{(X_{1}(t),\ldots,X_{r}(t))^{*}\}_{t\in{\mathbb{Z}}}
is a
r
-variate stochastic process, where the entry-wise processes are independent and
X_{\ell}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textnormal{f.d.d.}}}{{=}}{\mathcal{X}}_{\tilde{d}_{\ell},k_{\ell}}
,
\ell=1,\ldots,r
;

(ii)

conditions
(W1-W3)
hold with
(1+\beta)/2-\alpha<\tilde{d}_{\ell}\leq N_{\psi}
and
k_{\ell}\leq N_{\psi}
,
\ell=1,\ldots,r
;

(iii)

\{a(n)\}_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}
is a dyadic sequence such that
n/a(n)\rightarrow\infty
and
(n/a(n))^{1/2}a(n)^{1-2\alpha-2\tilde{d}_{1}}\rightarrow 0
, as
n\rightarrow\infty
.

Then, with
{\mathbf{H}}=\textnormal{diag}(\tilde{d}_{1}-\frac{1}{2},\ldots,\tilde{d}_{r}-\frac{1}{2})
the weak limit (
2.30
) holds for some diagonal matrix
\Sigma_{B}(j_{1},\ldots,j_{m})
. Moreover,

\|{{\mathbf{B}}}_{a}(2^{j})-\mathbf{B}(2^{j})\|=O(a(n)^{-\beta}),\quad n\to\infty,\quad j=j_{1},\ldots,j_{m}.

(C.52)

In particular, conditions (
2.30
) and (
2.31
) in assumption (A3) hold.

Proof:

The limit (
2.30
) is an immediate consequence of Theorem 2 in Roueff and Taqqu
[
roueff:taqqu:2009
]
. For the limit (
C.52
), observe that

{\mathbb{E}}\mathbf{W}_{X}(2^{j})=\textnormal{diag}\big({\mathbb{E}}D_{X}(2^{j},0)_{1}^{2},\ldots,{\mathbb{E}}D_{X}(2^{j},0)_{r}^{2}\big).

Moreover, in view of condition (
C.50
), under
(i)
,
(ii)
and
(iii)
, expression (67) in Roueff and Taqqu
[
roueff:taqqu:2009
]
shows that, for some constants
K_{\ell}(j)
,

\big|a(n)^{-2\tilde{d}_{\ell}}{\mathbb{E}}D_{X}(a(n)2^{j},0)_{\ell}^{2}-K_{\ell}(j)\big|=O(a(n)^{-\beta}),\qquad\ell=1,\ldots,r.

Therefore, with
{\mathbf{H}}=\textnormal{diag}(\tilde{d}_{1}-\frac{1}{2},\ldots,\tilde{d}_{r}-\frac{1}{2})
,

\|{{\mathbf{B}}}_{a}(2^{j})-\mathbf{B}(2^{j})\|

=\big\|\textnormal{diag}\big(a(n)^{-2\tilde{d}_{1}}{\mathbb{E}}D_{X}(a(n)2^{j},0)_{1}^{2}-K_{1}(j),

\ldots,a(n)^{-2\tilde{d}_{r}}{\mathbb{E}}D_{X}(a(n)2^{j},0)_{r}^{2}-K_{r}(j)\big)\big\|=O(a(n)^{-\beta}),

which shows (
C.52
).
\Box
